All of a sudden a bird calls out in a shrill voice. From the corner of your\neye, you notice movement amid the tall grass in the distance.\nYou know you are in danger. You are being watched. It is a tiger waiting to\npounce. You run, but the tiger gives chase. Within moments you come across a\ndeep, yawning pit. If you want to escape the tiger, you have no choice but to hurl\nyourself down below. The fall is treacherous, the terrain rugged and steep, and as\nyou tumble down toward the murky water, bruised and bloody, you look down\nand see a giant crocodile with its jaws wide open.\nTerri\u0000ed, you try to cling to the walls of the pit. After a few failed attempts,\nyou manage to grab onto a wild creeping vine, your feet dangling a few feet from\nthe open mouth of the crocodile. With tiger above and crocodile below, you are\nholding on for dear life. That’s when you notice two mice, one white and one\nblack, nibbling away at the very creeper you’re holding on to. Suspended in this\nstate of terror, you feel something drip onto your head. You crane your head\nupward and notice a honeycomb at a great height above you. Longing for a\nsweet moment of relief, you stretch out your tongue and wait for the next drop\nof honey to land on your tongue.\nFirst of all, we apologize for having subjected you to this terrifying journey of\nimagination! But please bear with us as we explain its purpose as we see it, for\ncomprehension of this ancient tale will help open your consciousness to a new\nway of thinking about abundance.\nThis story is a visual representation of how so many of us experience life. The\ntiger is the terrifying state that creeps in when an obsession with self is in\ncontrol: a deep fear that we do not matter. We call it the “Nobody State.”\nThe pit is our plunge into unconscious, aggressive, and ambitious pursuit of\nwealth creation. Rolling into the pit might seem like the way out of the Nobody\nState, but it’s a journey we’re undertaking out of fear, not a sense of joy or\npurpose.\nThe crocodile is the endless loop of \u0000nancial problems that erupt as a result\nof your self-obsession as well as the life of mediocrity that awaits you as you slide\ndown the pit.\nThe vine on which you are dangling is your hope.\nThe white and black mice nibbling at the vine are the passage of night and\nday with which your hope is thinning.\nAnd, \u0000nally, the honey represents a few moments of pleasure we yearn for\namid all the insecurity and chaos.\nHow does one escape such a predicament?\nWell, we know the path most frequently taken. The moment we see the tiger,\nwe do just what the character in the story did. We run head\u0000rst toward the pit—\nthough, of course, that pit can take any number of guises. It’s the job our\nparents wanted us to take. The industry that promises us the respect we never\nreceived as teenagers. The position that will make us richer than our siblings.\nThe status that will ensure that we are never discounted at a party.\nSuch a path might appear to o\u0000er achievement and \u0000nancial success. But if\nwe take any course of action merely to \u0000ll this su\u0000ering state of inner emptiness,\nour stress and anxiety have a way of creating a negative vortex of energy around\nus that invites greater chaos and the gnawing feeling of mediocrity. When we live\nfrom such an inferior state of consciousness, we hurt every other aspect of life.\nThis certainly is not the path to \u0000nancial abundance. It is not the state from\nwhich conscious wealth creation can happen.\nBut there is a greater path. There is another state of consciousness.\nLife in the Nobody State\nMike, CEO of a highly successful architecture \u0000rm, still remembers the moment\nhe decided no one would ever look down on him again. He was a young teenager\nwhen it happened, but the shameful feeling of being ditched by the girl he loved\nfor a rich kid remained with him long after.\nHis \u0000rm’s climb to recognition was hard and painful, yet it did not bring him\nany of the happiness he had hoped it would. After many bitter years of striving,\nhe gave in to a state of arrogance. To escape the feeling of inadequacy, he spent a\nlot of time telling himself and others what a great man he was.\nWhen Mike was in his late forties, driven by jealousy and unable to bear the\nrise of a competitor in his \u0000eld, he made some sly moves to bring down his rival.\nBut his strategy back\u0000red in a massive way. A series of events unfolded that\ntarnished his image in the media. He lost his company, and his clients moved on\nto other \u0000rms. His employees left him to start o\u0000 on their own.\nAs Mike re\u0000ected upon two decades of work down the drain, he knew he had\nfallen as far down the pit as possible. It was time to stop running.\nMike was a reluctant participant at a four-day course we were o\u0000ering. He\nwas only there because his teenage daughter had insisted!\nOn the second day, however, he really began to look within, and it dawned on\nhim that what he had always seen as focus and drive was really an addiction. His\npainful pursuit of wealth and success had been a futile attempt to \u0000ll the inner\nvoid that he had \u0000rst begun to experience as a teen.\nHe had never addressed this nagging feeling of inadequacy, so it only grew\nwith the passing years. The only means he had to silence it were his attempts to\nbecome bigger than everyone else in his \u0000eld. The result was a rush toward\nsuccess built on aggression and ruthlessness.\nHe realized that inwardly he felt just as hollow and unimportant as a\nsuccessful executive as he had as a teen. It didn’t matter how many people\nworked under him or how highly he was regarded in his industry. He still felt\ninsigni\u0000cant the moment he even imagined somebody would grow bigger than\nhim. He still su\u0000ered.\nOn the \u0000nal day of the course, during a mystic journey into the Limitless\nField meditation with me, he was gripped by a paralyzing fear: the fear of dying a\nnobody. He could not shake the fear no matter how hard he tried to redirect his\nthinking.\nIt was only when he surrendered to the realization that his life had not really\nmattered to anyone that he saw a humbling truth: he had made no one’s life\nricher because of his presence in it.\nIn a deep meditation, he allowed the pain of a purposeless existence to set in.\nIn a moment a great heat arose in his navel region. He felt as if a great \u0000re were\nconsuming that all-too-familiar sensation of discontent within his heart and gut.\nAfter about an hour, a sweet sleep overcame him.\nMike’s ride back on the plane was a life review. He saw the futility of\npursuing wealth from this Nobody State. None of his material success had done\na thing to make him feel whole.\nHis process of authentic transformation had begun. A new life beckoned,\nbut he had several decisions to make. Would he retire and pursue some hobby or\nstart a new career? Would he continue with his old profession? Would he build\nhis career again in the same city or relocate? Would he work alone or together\nwith others? Where would he begin?\nWe will get back to his journey shortly.\nThe Pain of the “Perfect Self”\nFrom a Nobody State like the one Mike became conscious of, our e\u0000orts to\ncreate abundance are like a hen’s \u0000ight: limited and tiresome. The Nobody State\ncan lead to three di\u0000erent paths:\n1. We fail to attain the wealth we seek because we are operating from\nsuch an inferior state of intelligence.\n2. Even if we do achieve some success, the climb will be hard and painful,\nleaving us no room for ful\u0000llment or celebration.\n3. Our su\u0000ering states will create problems that put everything we’ve\nworked so hard for in jeopardy.\nIf each of those scenarios sounds like a dead end, let us put your mind at ease.\nThere is a reason the Nobody State has such control over us—and once we\nbecome aware of it, it begins to lose its power.\nBut just how did it get so strong in the \u0000rst place?\nRemember, our minds are not merely the result of the information we have\nconsciously chosen to feed it. Into it also pour the aspirations, prejudices, fears,\nand desires of our grandparents, parents, teachers, friends, high school\nsweethearts, partners—as well as the opinions of people we may not even like all\nthat much!\nThis collective in\u0000ow of information has crystallized in us an image of the\nPerfect Self. Unfortunately, this Perfect Self is emotionally virtuous,\nintellectually brilliant, physically attractive, and \u0000nancially abundant. Think\nSteve Jobs, Oprah Winfrey, Gigi Hadid, and Warren Bu\u0000ett all rolled into one\nperson—and don’t forget to add a dash of the Dalai Lama! If you look closely,\nthis is more or less the image of your ideal self.\nEven if we don’t consciously model ourselves after the world’s most\nadmirable, successful, or attractive individuals, we have been bombarded with\nmessages from day one about what it means to be good, happy, and successful.\nNaturally, an idea of the person we “should be” forms pretty early in life and\nbegins to become more and more complicated as we start to feel judged by\nfamily, friends, and teachers.\nWithout us realizing it, this Perfect Self becomes the inner gauge of who we\nshould be. We constantly measure this ideal against our reality, and each time we\nfall short, we feel disappointed in ourselves and our lives. We feel empty and\nbegin to chase all our goals from a place of despair.\nDoes what we are discussing sound to you like we are asking you to become\nmore “reasonable,” lower your expectations, or give up your desires in order to\nlive a contented life?\nWell, that is not our philosophy. For what is the yardstick for what is\nreasonable and unreasonable?\nNor do we believe in living a substandard life of compromises. We don’t\nbelieve that you have to shun desire. To us, what matters is the state from which\nwe pursue all our desires, be they grand or simple.\nSo what do we do when the Nobody State takes over? And what if it’s all\nwe’ve ever known?\nLet us tell you this: no one was born a nobody.\nBefore we started to experience this psychic split between the Perfect Self\nfantasy and the Nobody State reality, we were in a beautiful state of being. As\nyoung children, we weren’t thinking about becoming anything other than what\nwe were experiencing in any given moment. We were one with our state of being,\nwhether that meant being angry, being joyful, being jealous, being bored, being\nplayful.\nIn this state of being, we were totally, unabashedly ourselves. This beautiful\nstate of innocence was a kind of paradise. Unhappiness was like drops of water\nthat ran down our bodies.\nNo matter the color of our skin or eyes, we were unapologetically ourselves.\nWhether we knew the alphabet or the numeric tables or not, we were in a state\nof peace. We learned things in our own way at our own time. Each e\u0000ort was a\nunique creative act.\nAs we grew older, we traded this inner sense of ease for intricate systems of\nmeasurement that ensure we will never be satis\u0000ed. The further away from our\nPerfect Self we fall, the deeper we sink into the painful Nobody State. Each time\nlife hurts us, this Nobody State grows more powerful.\nWhen our parents compare us with a sibling, the fear of being a nobody\ncreeps in. When a teacher treats another kid better, the fear of being a nobody\nbites. When our crush rejects us or we fail to land our dream job, the fear of\nbeing a nobody begins to consume us.\nEven those who have achieved great fame experience this Nobody State. But\nif they stay in that place, even the most prestigious awards and accolades will\nbring them no closer to joy. Please know this: From this Nobody State, we are\nnot pursuing wealth creation. We are only addictively hurtling down the pit of\ndespair.\n\nPlease pause. Let us take a moment to be truthful about the way the Nobody State\ncan limit our ability to build wealth and experience abundance in its many forms.\nHow is the Nobody State keeping you stuck in despair? Remember, if you want the\nuniversal intelligence to present solutions, you can put aside your attachments and\nself-interest by practicing Serene Mind. (For a refresher on the practice, please see\npages 65–66.) See the situation from a vaster perspective. See the impact you can\nhave on the whole, be it your family, your organization, or your environment.\nSolutions will come to you as intuition or as inspiration in meditation or as an idea\nfrom an unexpected quarter.\nAs a conscious leader, ask yourself: What is the state that is powering my\nachievement? Am I using comparison and fear of being less as my fuel to rise? Or\nam I inspired by a state of deep and joyful passion to create a difference?\nHealing the Inﬂamed Mind\nWe’ve all experienced in\u0000ammation of the body. When something harmful or\nirritating tries to invade an organism, there is a biological response to try to\nremove it. The visible signs and symptoms of in\u0000ammation, while often painful\nor irritating, are evidence that the body is in a state of war while trying to heal\nitself.\nOf course, sometimes in\u0000ammation can upset the body’s equilibrium,\ncreating further in\u0000ammation. The danger of chronic, low-level in\u0000ammation is\nthat its silent nature hides its destructive power. In fact, stress-induced\nin\u0000ammation, once triggered, can persist undetected for years, even decades,\npropagating cell death throughout the body. This state of war in the body can\nmanifest itself as diabetes, Alzheimer’s, meningitis, cancer, or coronary heart\ndisease. This is why so much medical research today focuses on \u0000ghting\nin\u0000ammatory diseases.\nJust as the body can remain in\u0000amed for years, so can our beings. What\nbegins as a normal response to hurt feelings becomes a disease of the heart and\nmind. And once the in\u0000ammation of the mind sets in, it can warp our life,\nundetected, for decades.\nWe may claim to be over the trauma of our past—w e may have built lives that\nlook nothing like the ones we had as children—but we must confront the terror\nof being a nobody if we are to heal ourselves, welcome abundance, and live\nbeautiful lives. How?\nOne of the symptoms of the Nobody State is that it often goes hand in hand\nwith extreme relationships to wealth. People experiencing this su\u0000ering state\ntend to have either an obsessive \u0000xation with money, a total distaste for it, or an\noscillating combination of the two.\nLet us \u0000rst discuss an obsession with money. We all know one of the classic\nsigns of our body \u0000ghting o\u0000 infection: fever. Well, some of us experience\nsomething similar when it comes to dealing with the Nobody State.\nSeized by a kind of delirium, we become \u0000xated with amassing wealth and\nstatus. When this fever takes over, we can’t see reality clearly. We hallucinate\nwarped visions of the future: If only I had more money, I would have everything I\nwant: love, happiness, and power. To be sure, we may feel short but unsustainable\nbursts of energy if we are driven by such a vision. In the long run, though, it is\nhard to build anything solid when we are in this frame of mind. Our single-\nminded obsession makes us terri\u0000ed of failure and incapable of exploration and\ncreative solutions to our challenges and problems.\nMei, an ikebana teacher, lived in fear of not having enough money for old\nage. She had, in fact, spent years obsessing and calculating how much she had in\nthe bank—and how much more she could have. But no actions she took, no\namount she earned, brought her any closer to feeling ful\u0000lled or secure, and she\nspent long periods of time in depression.\nWhen she took a look at the roots of these feelings, she realized that as a child\nshe had felt that nothing she did was enough to please her mother. She took that\nsame state of mind into every one of her relationships with friends and crushes.\nEven back in high school, \u0000nancial abundance was an obsession. She hoped\nmoney would take away the painful state of insecurity she experienced within.\nWith the passing of time, Mei’s relationship with money became very\ndistorted. She would spend loads of time calculating how much she would have\nwhen she retired and panic that she did not have enough. This panic would drive\nher to work relentlessly and make more money. But she would also compulsively\nshop or make unwise investments all the time, regretting the money that was\n\u0000owing out of her hands. She lived in an inner state of poverty. Her panic would\nmount to the point that she would spend days at a time feeling sick and fatigued.\nMei’s liberation came when she realized that her current problems were\ncompletely a result of her inner state. It was not the universe that was testing her\nwith problems but her in\u0000amed mind that was propelling her life out of control.\nAs her awareness of her inner state deepened over the next months, she naturally\nawoke to a beautiful state of connection with the way of the \u0000owers and her\nikebana students.\nThe deep spiritual journey Mei has embarked upon has shifted the\nneurological circuits in her brain that once kept her anxious and obsessed about\n\u0000nances. She now largely lives in a beautiful state and feels the universe is more\nof an ally to her, o\u0000ering her many lucky breaks and synchronicities.\nJust as a fever can warn us that we have the \u0000u, our money mania can call our\nattention to some deeper ailment. Sure, we can stock up on medicine that hides\nthe signs of in\u0000ammation—or we can use our discomfort as a clarion call to face\nour Nobody State once and for all.\nThe second relationship with wealth we create from the Nobody State is\navoidance. If some of us respond to the Nobody State by rushing head\u0000rst into\nobsessing over our bank statements, then others run straight in the opposite\ndirection.\nMoney is, after all, evil, goes this line of thinking. All it’s ever done is make\npeople arrogant and the world bad. . . . There’s no point chasing it or respecting it.\nSuch inner disgust might even turn into anger we feel is justi\u0000ed: How can my\nneighbors have such a big house when so many in the world have nothing?\nWith this kind of an abnegating relationship, we do not respect the\ncontributions we make. We hesitate to ask for the true worth of our services. We\ncomplain that people are unfair in what they o\u0000er us but do not have the\ncourage to ask for what is our due.\nLook a little deeper, however, and we often \u0000nd that a distaste for money and\nthe people who have it also has its roots in some in\u0000ammation from the past.\nThis second obsession is as foolish and as dangerous a reaction as the \u0000rst one.\nAre we stuck there?\nPlease pause. If you are comfortable with a small inner journey, let us pause here.\nLet us ground ourselves in the present moment by slowing down our breath.\nLet’s delve deeper into our inner truth. Please sit still and observe. What is our\nrelationship with wealth? Do we obsess about it? Is it like a pebble in our shoe,\ndrawing our attention with every footstep we take? Or do we have indifference or\ndisdain toward wealth?\nHow often does the fear of being a nobody consume us? How often do we\nobsess about living and dying as someone of no consequence to anyone? Has an\nobsession with becoming a somebody in the future stopped you from living,\nconnecting, and feeling? Or is your pursuit of abundance born from a joyful state of\nself-discovery and a desire to share your gifts with the world?\nThe point is not to admonish ourselves for the su\u0000ering state of fear. Quite\nthe contrary: Let us congratulate ourselves, for a journey from untruth to truth\nis the key to a beautiful state!\nWe can become free of this Nobody State. In fact, we must. Such a state of\nsu\u0000ering has a way of creating a kind of negative energy \u0000eld around us.\nWe’ve all witnessed this, haven’t we? We’ve seen someone in the throes of\n\u0000nancial obsession making terribly unwise decisions that a\u0000ect everyone they\ncome into contact with. We’ve seen how the Nobody State manifests as\nworkaholism or depression. It’s not easy to be around someone in that state.\nThey are often too consumed with feelings of rage or shame to feel anyone’s\nlove.\nAt the macro level, the Nobody State becomes a force that stops wealth from\n\u0000owing into us. It prevents intelligence. It prevents auspiciousness. It closes the\ndoor on Lakshmi, the goddess of wealth in Hindu culture, from entering our\nlife.\nBut we can break free of it.\nRemember Mike, the CEO of the architecture \u0000rm? His inward journey did\nnot result in him giving up his pursuit of \u0000nancial security. But he did give up\nsomething else. His pursuit no longer came from a place of emptiness. It was no\nlonger driven by anger at all those who had mocked him and pulled out of\npartnerships with him. It did not even come from a desperation to rebuild his\nlost image in society.\nMike awakened to a beautiful state of calm courage. He was conscious of the\ndevastation his own state and actions had caused him. He had no ax to grind.\nThe power of his transformed consciousness led him to a new purpose: a passion\nto utilize his knowledge for the greater good.\nToday a new team has gathered around him. As we write these words, he and\nhis team are engaged in providing architectural solutions online to aspiring\nlearners. Established in the beautiful states of peace and passion, they are\nbuilding their dream brick by brick. This time the climb upward feels inwardly\ne\u0000ortless.\nBecoming a Conscious Creator\nWealth creation is one of the most discussed topics in the world. You have, no\ndoubt, heard of many techniques and strategies about how to grow rich.\nThe journey to becoming a conscious wealth creator is very di\u0000erent.\nWe advocate for a consciousness approach to creating abundance—a step\naway from the destructive states that hold us back from manifesting our dreams\nand a leap toward a consciousness that is wildly creative and fully awake. No\nlonger do you need to create, build, and achieve from a place of lack. You will\nlearn how to draw from a much deeper well of creativity.\nWhen students take this journey in consciousness, they awaken to exciting\nnew opportunities and experience miraculous synchronicities. Instead of\n\u0000ghting against the great current of life, a great \u0000ow carries them past many\nwondrous banks on the river of life.\nSo Who Is a Conscious Wealth Creator?\nA conscious wealth creator is aware of the state from which they pursue wealth and\nsuccess.\nA conscious wealth creator is aware of the purpose behind their pursuit.\nA conscious wealth creator is cognizant of the impact their wealth creation has\non the ecosystem that surrounds them.\nLet me now share an experience of a well-known CEO from a much-loved\norganization.\nSome years ago, this newly appointed CEO and his wife went through his\n\u0000rst journey of transformation with the academy.\nThe CEO’s company was in a state of \u0000nancial crisis and at the meeting of\nthe board of directors, the onus was laid on him to turn it around.\nHe decided what most leaders would decide when challenged with such a\ncrisis: to let go of people in order to cut costs.\nWith a very heavy heart he told his wife his plan for layo\u0000s—and he was met\nwith a question.\n“I understand you have decided to let go of people,” she said, “but from\nwhich state are you making this decision? Are you deciding from fear or love?”\nThe CEO began his journey with connecting to the \u0000rst sacred secret of\nspiritual vision. He became conscious that he was respond-ing to his challenge\nfrom a state of su\u0000ering and not a beautiful state. He knew with total certainty\nthat his su\u0000ering only made him desperate and stupid. His su\u0000ering state was\ndrawing more problems to him. Established in a vision to dissolve su\u0000ering and\naddress his company’s challenge from a beautiful state, the CEO journeyed into\nthe second sacred secret of inner truth. He realized that his decision was fueled\nmore from a space of self-protection and self-obsession than from a larger vision\nfor the organization, and that he was deciding from a place of fear. He feared\nhow the board would perceive him and he was desperate to prove himself\nsuccessful in the eyes of his directors.\nOnce he recognized his fear, he asked himself: In which state do I want to\nrespond to this situation?\nThe answer was in front of him. As he continued to meditate, he envisioned\nmoving into a space of connection with all his employees. He felt what they\nwould feel if they lost their jobs right before Christmas.\nHe then followed the fourth sacred secret of spiritual right action. With clear\nresolve and trust in the universe, he inspired his entire company to come\ntogether as a family and save on costs at every level, from production and\npackaging to transportation and showcasing.\nHis state of connection a\u0000ected everyone at the organization on a very deep\nlevel. They came together as one to make this happen and the company stayed\na\u0000oat.\nAnd other shifts started to \u0000ow in his favor.\nAs Christmas approached, the economy turned around. Demand increased—\ndemand the company could meet, but only because they had not let go of sta\u0000.\nDuring his tenure there, his company achieved unparalleled growth year after\nyear.\nFour Pursuits, Two Paths\nEven though we do not personally like \u0000aunting wealth—or \u0000aunting anything,\nfor that matter!—we would like to share the wisdom that has guided us as we’ve\ncreated successful international businesses together over the course of our\ntwenty-two-year marriage and has guided many of our students to become\nconscious wealth creators.\nAccording to the ancient Indian sages, all of humanity’s longings can be\nclassi\u0000ed into four pursuits:\n• Artha—w ealth and all the comforts and luxuries wealth can bring\n• Kama—love in all its forms as a\u0000ection, intimacy, respect, and compassion\n• Dharma—th e passion to make a di\u0000erence to your family, your\norganization, and the world\n• Mukti—a spiritual awakening, also called enlightenment, in which you are\nfree of su\u0000ering and the illusion of separation\nNo matter what our desires, each can be categorized into one of these four\nprime pursuits. Even though this was the prevalent framework in the culture we\nboth grew up in, it was only after we began dreaming up the curriculum for\nO&O Academy that we had a greater realization about this ancient wisdom:\nEach of these four goals—and, by extension, all human longings—can be\npursued either from a beautiful state or from a su\u0000ering Nobody State.\nLet us explore what we mean by looking \u0000rst at Dharma.\nWe all have responsibilities: as children to our parents, companions to our\npartners, citizens to our communities. But when we perform our roles from a\nstressed state, they become chores and burdens we must bear. We perform our\nduties because we are driven by ideals or because we are trying to play our parts\nin a system. But there is no heart in duty.\nWhen we move into a beautiful state, the essence of which is connection,\nhowever, our Dharma becomes a passion to nurture the fabric of our family,\ncommunity, and society. We utilize our skills, our in\u0000uence, and all we have for\nthe well-being of the larger whole. We recognize the interconnectedness of all\nthings and recognize that our state and our actions have wide-reaching e\u0000ects on\nthe web of life. When we consider the ripple e\u0000ect we naturally create, could any\none of us really be a nobody?\nLikewise, Kama—the quest for love—can be pursued from this Nobody\nState. When that happens, the desire for love becomes a never-ending longing.\nWe desperately try to please others or look to others to please us. This desperate\npursuit of pleasure becomes an uncontrolled obsession.\nContrast this with Kama pursued from a beautiful state, which results in a\nlove that is nurturing, uplifting, and liberating.\nWhen even Mukti, or spiritual seeking, is born of the Nobody State, it\nbecomes an ambitious and aggressive process of accumulating knowledge and\nspiritual skills for display. We try to escape our real-life challenges by clinging to a\nlofty self-image of otherworldliness—and as a result \u0000nd ourselves wandering\ndeeper down the path of despair, isolation, and con\u0000ict. This is why even very\npious individuals can feel like they are at war with the world and why some of\nthe most generous among us can’t quiet that little voice crying, Why does\neveryone have it so much easier?\nA classic example of someone who pursued a spiritual path from a Nobody\nState was a cousin of the Buddha named Devadatta. Devadatta was handsome\nand brilliant, and sometimes acknowledged to be even more skilled in speech\nthan the Buddha.\nThe story goes that when both were just children, a swan that Devadatta shot\nfell at the feet of the Buddha; the Buddha promptly proceeded to tend to its\nwounds and nurse it back to health. Devadatta claimed the swan to be his since\nhe shot it, but the elders decided to give the swan to the Buddha since he gave it\nlife.\nPerhaps that was the beginning of the Nobody State in Devadatta, or perhaps\nit began with some other event even before that. When the Buddha \u0000nally\nreturned home after his enlightenment, Devadatta joined his monastery—but\nonly because he harbored the secret desire of proving himself a greater teacher.\nHe performed great austerities but died a discontented man.\nAs much as we would all like to be the Buddha in this particular story, it’s not\nthat di\u0000cult to empathize with Devadatta. Who among us has not felt such\njealousy when we’ve encountered greatness, especially in our kin and close\nfriends? Who among us has not waged an internal war against a sibling or a\nfriend who seemed to have caught all the breaks we never did?\nThis is the unfortunate force of the Nobody State—and why we cannot\nunderestimate it. Its downward pull is very seductive.\nWhich brings us to the fourth pursuit. Just as Dharma should not be\napproached as a burden, abundance does not come by mindlessly obsessing\nabout the acquisition of Artha, or wealth.\nThe higher pursuit of Artha—conscious wealth creation—is only possible\nfrom a beautiful state. In a beautiful state we are not driven by the obsession to\nwin all the time or the anxiety of losing. Wealth creation is no longer a war to us.\nSuccess is no longer a matter of life and death. Our journey to achievement\nbecomes playful. In such a consciousness there is an explosion of creativity.\nWealth comes searching for us.\nFrom this connected and creative state, we awaken to a greater purpose, one\nwe hope will outlive us and our generation. It begins with seeing our\nintelligence, our capacities, and our talents not merely as tools to expand our\nin\u0000uence or a\u0000uence but rather as a means for transforming a person, a\nsituation, or the world around us.\nBut what do we do when we feel like we do have a purpose—but it is not\nenough to free us from stress and su\u0000ering?\nPursuing Life’s Purpose from a Beautiful State of\nConnection\nLet me share with you the story of a young Korean gentleman who had built a\ncompany dedicated to improving the lives of animals. He came to us feeling\ndepressed and suicidal because he believed he was failing as a leader.\nMany of us think the cause of unhappiness at work is a lack of purpose. But\nhe had a purpose: he had always wanted to use technology to make the lives of\nanimals better, and he pioneered this technology while still in college.\nOr perhaps we think unhappiness stems from a failure to execute one’s\npurpose. But this man’s company was successful—and growing.\nPerhaps, then, we think the cause of unhappiness is a bad corporate hierarchy.\nBut nothing was lacking in that area for our student.\nSo what was the cause of his pain? Why did he feel like a failure in spite of\ndeveloping a growing business aligned with his purpose?\nHe told us more about his situation as he went deeper into his journey with\nus. He said that in the past \u0000ve years nearly one hundred of his employees had\ndecided to leave the company. He kept them at the company with a lot of\npersuasion. But he wasn’t sure he could continue pushing. He was tired. Tired\nof pushing, incentivizing, and persuading.\nOn a spiritual journey at the academy, he realized that his fatigue came not\nbecause of work but because of his inner state. Inside, he was like a man who’d\nstepped onto a treadmill and could not step o\u0000: he kept trying to prove his self-\nworth to his dad, trying not to feel second in the company to the smarter\nexecutives who joined, trying to gain the respect of the directors in every\nmeeting.\nHe saw that all his achievements had been driven by a fear of not measuring\nup. He was a disconnected human being who did not actually respect his\nemployees. His relationship with them was merely transactional.\nAs he moved inward, this man awoke to a state of gratitude for all the\nemployees who were supporting his growth and success. Their frustrations and\ntheir discontent \u0000nally began to matter to him.\nNine months after his transformational journey, he said there was already a\nchange in the current of the organization as, bit by bit, he helped usher the\nentire team into a space of greater harmony.\nAs this story reveals, even a deep sense of purpose can be undercut by\nstressful states. When your work, your career, and your goals are driven by\nstressful states, your career becomes a war zone of the destructive unconscious.\nWhen your work is inspired by a beautiful state, on the other hand, your\nwork becomes a playing \u0000eld of the presence of the universal intelligence.\nYour state of being is like the horse that lugs a cart in the direction it chooses\nto go. The cart could be your career, your relationships at work, or the impact\nyou are having on the entire ecosystem.\nYour states lead the way and your life follows.\nWhere Are You Going?\nLet us ponder some essential questions: Where are you going? And what is\ndirecting you there? From which state are you leading your team? From which\nstate is your manager leading their team? What do you want the culture of your\norganization to be as you walk through its doors day after day? A stressful state\nor a beautiful state? A connected state or a disconnected state?\nPlease pause. At this moment, what is driving your career decision? Are you\nfollowing your anxiety? Is work merely a means of survival? Would you quit your\njob in a heartbeat if you had the money?\nOr are you following your frustration and anger? Is your work a way to establish\na sense of self-worth to yourself, your partner, your parent, your sibling, your\nenemy, or your followers?\nPerhaps you are following your boredom instead. Is work merely an escape\nfrom boredom, a way to kill time?\nWhat are you following?\nWhere are you trying to go?\nPlease see your inner truth without resisting it.\nWhat did the last moment of re\u0000ection reveal? Are you following your joy,\nyour gratitude, or your compassion? Or have you let fear and anxiety hijack your\npath to abundance?\nWhen you awaken to wealth consciousness, you enjoy the entire process of\ncreation. You are conscious of the impact your service is making on the lives of\nothers. You are conscious of the impact the work and service of others is having\non you.\nBecause whether you’ve thought about it or not, it does have an impact.\nLet me share a story I heard that re\u0000ects this powerful truth.\nA man was driving his car one day. He took the same twisting road he always\ntook, but this time his car hit a large rock that had rolled into the road. He spun\no\u0000 th e road, crashed, and died.\nBut the story does not end there. Thanks to the miracles of modern science,\nthe ICU revived him—and the moment he woke up, he was a completely\ndi\u0000erent person.\nWhat happened?\nIn those moments before he was revived, he saw his entire life play itself out\nin front of his eyes. The only di\u0000erence was that he saw it from the outside—\nfrom the perspectives of every living creature he’d ever come into contact with.\nHe saw himself as a small child hitting a goat with a stick, but this time he\nexperienced the same shock and pain the goat experienced. He saw himself\nbullying other children in school, but this time it was their humiliation and fear\nthat he felt. As he saw the entirety of his life play out before him like a movie in\nwhich he was often the villain, a great sadness engulfed him.\nThere has always been a wall between myself and others, he realized. And that\nwall has been me.\nHe fell into a deep state of su\u0000ering as he re\u0000ected upon his wasted life.\nThen another series of memories unfolded before him. Many mornings as he\ndrove to work over the same rocky patch where he’d lost control of his car, there\nwould be turtles trying to cross the road. The man knew the turtles would be\nkilled by speeding cars if someone didn’t intervene. And so he would park his car\nand pick the turtles up and drop them in the direction they were attempting to\ngo before continuing his drive to work.\nOf course, as this memory played out before him as he lay unconscious, the\nman wasn’t just seeing himself performing this small act of kindness: he was the\nturtle being carried, cradled, and taken to safety. In that moment his heart\nopened to the experience of love. He felt deeply connected to all of life. He\nrealized that every time we hurt one another, we hurt the entire fabric of life.\nEvery time we love and care for one another, we nurture that fabric.\nAt the moment this realization came, he was pulled back into his body. He\nknew at once that he had a di\u0000erent life to live, a di\u0000erent experience to give to\nthe world.\nWhile the transformations in consciousness that people experience when they\nvisit our academy are all unique, many students have shared with us insights that\nare remarkably similar to this man’s near-death experience. When people have a\ndirect experience of an expanded state of consciousness, it’s common for their\nentire perception of the world to be transformed.\nFor the \u0000rst time, they have been given a much more expansive\nunderstanding of their lives, the ripple e\u0000ects of their actions, and the rich web\nof life that supports and sustains us all.\nAwakening to Interconnection\nWe live in a world that is interconnected.\nOur actions matter.\nIt has taken the labor and insights of millions of people over the years for you\nand me to experience one comfortable day. You and I cannot enjoy one good\nmeal but for the tireless e\u0000orts of millions. And every one of us is one of those\nmillions who keep this world alive! Every morning when we leave home for\nwork, we are actually serving a mission called “Harmonious World.”\nEvery time you type something on your Mac . . . every time you wear that\nspecially designed suit to perform risky lab work . . . every time you contemplate\na new business opportunity . . . every time you turn to page sixty-three to read a\npoem to the students in your classroom . . . every time you bring those three\nhundred passengers on board safely to their desired destinations . . . every time\nyou engage in any of those myriad other forms of work . . . you are actually\nhelping the world stay in harmony, and you become invaluable to the\nsustainability and harmony of this beautiful earth.\nWhen we awaken to this state of interconnectedness, our e\u0000ciency has a way\nof skyrocketing. We enjoy great success both on a personal level and in terms of\nour contribution to the success of every member of our organizations and the\nworld.\nOne of our students was a stylist at a fancy salon. She often moved into a\ndullness and felt a sense of meaninglessness at the end of each day. She\nentertained her clients with clever talk, but she felt empty. Why did day after day\ncoloring and cutting hair feel the same?\nAfter we took her through one of our processes, she was able to awaken to a\ndeep state of love. As a result, her experience of her work underwent a total\ntransformation. Today she connects to the inner states of her clients. She thinks\nabout the state of joy a single working mother feels for days after she styles her\nhair. She re\u0000ects upon the con\u0000dence a teenager feels after he walks into his \u0000rst\nday of college with a fresh look. Our student connects to her clients deeply.\nWhat began as a career for survival has now become a conscious act of love to\nimpact people.\nBut she has not stopped there. The truth was she wanted to expand her role\nbeyond that of stylist—and some of her dissatisfaction was a wake-up call that\nshe wanted more. Her experience also gave her the burst of courage she needed\nto launch her own line of earth-friendly products.\nWhen we pursue a conscious path of wealth creation, we awaken to love for\nthe people we work with and the people we a\u0000ect through our work. Our heart\nshould wake up. For if we do not care for what the other feels, we may brush\nshoulders with them and work together, but we fundamentally feel alone and\nstuck in our stressful states. It is only when we feel connected to another that we\nexperience feelings of security, safety, and nurturing.\nPerhaps you still have doubts about whether the state of connection is all it’s\ncracked up to be. If so, you’re not alone.\n“Why is it important to connect to another human being?” was a question\nposed to us by a student on his \u0000rst day at the academy.\nScott was young and successful. At age thirty-two he had already risen to a\nvery high managerial position at his organization.\nHe considered himself a self-made man.\n“I can connect to this table here, my favorite car, my personal skills and\nabilities,” he continued. “Why should I depend on a human being who is likely\nto change anytime? I only rely on me, my capabilities, and the things I love.”\nLater in the day he mentioned that he had come to our campus because he\nwas feeling lost in life. He woke up most days with three questions.\n1. What is the point of continuing to put my energy and creativity into\nthis organization?\n2. Why am I doing all this?\n3. And for whose sake am I doing it? The team I’ve spent so long\nnurturing does not seem to need me anymore.\nAs Scott journeyed inward, he realized that all his hurts over the years had\nmade him reactive. He saw the way he held on to disappointments with people\nas a way of disconnecting from them.\nDid he want to be a disconnected person for the rest of his life?\nAs Scott made the choice of letting go of his anger and disappointments, his\nperception of himself and his life began to change. He could no longer see\nhimself as a self-made person. He began to think about the many people who\nhad played a role in his rise. He began to re\u0000ect on all the ways his team supports\nhim through acts both large and small.\nAs his consciousness expanded, he felt a deep desire take root within him for\nhis team and his organization to be happier.\nSix months after Scott had completed his course in India, he told us he\nrediscovered his sense of purpose.\n“Going to work and growing it is a joy now,” he said. “It is like my brain has\nentered a space of creativity. Synchronicities abound around me.”\nIf you are feeling a loss of purpose, there is a huge chance it is a loss of\nconnection. By awakening to a state of heartfelt connection, you will inevitably\nawaken to a greater sense of purpose. You will realize what it means to truly\ncooperate.\nWhy is this kind of heartfelt connection necessary in organizations? And how\ncan leaders nurture it?\nHeartfelt connection is not simply an attitude or an activity you do together.\nIt is a state of consciousness in which you see your well-being as inseparable from\nthe well-being of others. It is the fusion of the two that makes a\nbeautiful life.\nOn a humorous note, let me put it this way: “To be a Buddha and driving a\nBenz with your loved ones” sums up a total life. What we mean by this is not\nthat every one of us ought to lean toward luxury, but rather that we can learn to\nlive in a beautiful state of consciousness and access the power of that state to\ncreate prosperity and love for ourselves, our loved ones, and the world around\nus.\nThe four sacred secrets are about this grand vision. We believe these teachings\nwill lead to the emergence of conscious wealth creators, awakened parents,\nheartful partners, and joyful individuals who live and function from a\ntransformed state of consciousness.\nPersonally, Preethaji and I have a rich and ful\u0000lling relationship with each\nother and Lokaa, our daughter. We have deep care and respect for our parents\nand our in-laws. We love mentoring our team of faculty members and the\nthousands of our students at the academy. These relationships and many others\nare rich not because of some ideals and values but because of the state of\nconsciousness in which we live. Su\u0000ering does not take root in our\nconsciousness.\nWe have amazing teams and trustworthy business partners and CEOs who\nsupport us in running our businesses while allowing us to pursue our mission of\ntransforming human consciousness. And the universe has been very benevolent\nwhen it comes to the many synchronicities it has brought our way. All this is\nhappening not because we are following some secret management principles; it is\nthe result of our state of consciousness.\nThe purpose of the four sacred secrets is to bring a rich internal state together\nwith a rich external life, for we want to make this complete life possible for all.\nQuestion: What do people learn at your academy?\nAnswer: O&O Academy is a philosophy and meditation school for transforming\nhuman consciousness, with its headquarters in India. We o\u0000er courses for people\nof di\u0000erent age groups from all nations in their respective languages. We have a\ncurriculum that spans several levels of learning and faculty who have dedicated\ntheir lives to creating this transformation in the students.\nOf course, most people \u0000rst engage with the academy outside India. Preethaji\ntravels to major cities around the world, o\u0000ering Field of Abundance, a four-\nday spiritual event, and she also teaches two-day online events: Source &\nSynchronicities and Being Limitless.\nTo know more about our courses, please visit www.oo.academy.\nQuestion: What draws people to your academy?\nAnswer: The ancient sages of India exhort us to become Dwijas, or the twice-\nborn, which means to become awakened individuals with a transformed\nconsciousness: those who purge themselves of their limiting conditioning\no\u0000ered by life and wake up to a consciousness that is limitless in its potential.\nIs such an awakening in the cards for everyone? Yes. Every ancient culture\nspeaks of profound spiritual journeys—and many have left the secrets of such\nadventures behind for posterity as symbols, mythology, sacred art and\narchitecture. Think for a moment about Odysseus returning to Ithaca after the\nfall of Troy, Jonah’s dive deep into the belly of the whale, Arjuna’s crisis of faith\nas he prepared himself for war, or the Chinese myth about the snake that entered\na dark cave, only to emerge in daylight as a dragon. Such stories are not mere\nentertainment; they are blueprints for transformation, and they contain within\nthem powerful wisdom.\nRites of passage have been key to the evolution of so many cultures—and yet,\nmany of us have lost connection to the transformative and healing power of\nthese trials by \u0000re. When disaster occurs or when life slowly and gradually turns\nbitter and disappointing—because of the death of a parent, the loss of love, the\ngradual dissolution of a dream—w e experience the same su\u0000ering that humans\nhave endured since the beginning of time. But for all our awe-inspiring\ntechnological advancements, modern society has not given us the necessary tools\nto emerge from these crises with a grander state of consciousness that we need\nfor the next chapter of life.\nOur processes are designed to help people move through each stage of life\nmore harmoniously, to awaken the potential for deep transformation that we all\npossess. We often say that the curriculum we teach and lead people through at\nthe academy transforms them into individuals who can extricate themselves\nfrom the helpless and random turning of the wheel of fortune and create a new\ndestiny for themselves. The practices we share will help you look at your life,\nyour relationships, and your habits with a new kind of sight.\nSince we opened our academy’s doors, we’ve helped hundreds of thousands\nof people not only answer fundamental questions of life but to live lives only a\nfew would have thought possible. We’ve taught students who are twelve and\nthose who are eighty-one. There are those who hail from South Korea, from\nNorthern California, and every place in between.\nSome have a dream they hope to realize or a painful experience they hope to\nlet go of. Some grapple with big questions: What does it mean to truly love\noneself or another? What is it to truly be alive? Does this universe have a\nconsciousness? Do I hold the power to change the course of my life?\nOthers grapple with big decisions: Should I stay in a relationship or not?\nMove to a new city? Leave my job for an exciting but uncertain opportunity?\nYet others are seekers endeavoring to set themselves totally free of su\u0000ering\nand the illusion of separation.\nThere are also seekers who come to us aspiring to have a direct experience of\nthe universal intelligence or the source.\nPeople come to us for many di\u0000erent reasons, but all of them are seekers\nyearning for something that seems ever so slightly out of their grasp. Every one\nof them is asking a variation of the same basic question: How can I attain that\nelusive thing I seek? Of course, these are not questions we answer for anyone—\nbut these are questions we help you \u0000nd the answers to for yourselves. These are\nquests we help you ful\u0000ll through penetrating insights, powerful mystical\nprocesses, and simple meditations.\nQuestion: What is your take on God?\nAnswer: God is a subjective experience. Di\u0000erent people have di\u0000erent\nde\u0000nitions of God. We help seekers realize their perception of God. Thereafter,\n“God” is no longer just a word to them.\nAnd depending on one’s culture, some experience this presence as a being\nthey have a personal relationship with, while some experience the universal\nintelligence as love, benevolence, and power. The more we live in beautiful\nstates, the greater our connection to the universal intelligence that \u0000ows into our\nlives. There are numerous processes at the academy designed to help people\nawaken to this presence.\nQuestion: So what is consciousness?\nAnswer: Consciousness is all that is. There is nothing that is not consciousness.\nYou, too, are of it. You are in it. You are it. Consciousness is both that which is\ncomprehensible to logic and that which is in the realm of the mystic.\nConsciousness is matter and your experience of that matter.\nSorry if this sounds too woo-woo. Words are indeed weak substitutes for the\nmystical; however, let us try to put it this way. If the sun rising were the physical\naspect of consciousness, its beauty and splendor or the lack thereof is the\nexperiential aspect of our consciousness. Let us say your newborn baby is the\nphysical aspect of consciousness; the feeling of love or the terror of responsibility\nwhen you hold the baby is the experiential aspect of consciousness.\nThe universe we experience with the \u0000ve senses is the physical aspect of\nconsciousness, while your subjective internal experience of it is the experiential\ndimension of consciousness. Science predominantly is involved in the\nexploration of the physical aspect of consciousness, while authentic spirituality is\ninvolved in the exploration and transformation of the internal or the experiential\ndimension of consciousness. The fundamental essence of transformation is to\nmove from a self-obsessed state of being we call I-consciousness to One-\nconsciousness.\nQuestion: Can you share more about the states of I-consciousness and One-\nconsciousness you just referred to?\nAnswer: If you looked at the ancient myths of the world—both Eastern and\nWestern—th ey show a war. A war between the gods and the demons. A war\nbetween light and darkness. In these stories sometimes the gods win, sometimes\nthe demons win.\nSometimes this war is happening in some heaven; sometimes this war is\nhappening on earth or in some netherworld. But what is this war? And where is\nthis war really taking place? This war is actually happening in our consciousness.\nConsciousness to us is a spectrum. On one end is what we refer to as I-\nconsciousness and on the other end is One-consciousness.\nI-consciousness is when thinking begins to revolve around ourselves\nobsessively. Thought begins to \u0000xate itself around me, me, me . . . our worries,\nour anxieties, our righteousness, our pleasures, our desires. It is a state of self-\nengrossment. This end of the spectrum of consciousness is the breeding ground\nfor destructive states of being such as discontent, anger, hate, fear, pain, and the\ndesire to control and dominate. I-consciousness is the dynamic of all su\u0000ering\nstates. Our sense of who we are becomes extremely limited. If our sense of self\nwere to be seen as a circle, in I-consciousness, neither our families, our children,\nnor our friends exist. Nothing exists. No one truly matters to us when we are\nstuck in su\u0000ering states. It is a very narrow and limited existence, and a painful\none. Everything shrinks in this state. Our creativity dries up, capacity declines,\nwealth diminishes, and relationships become fragile. We feel as if the universe is\nagainst us.\nFrom I-consciousness we unconsciously and impulsively perform actions that\nwill lead us and others to pain and loss.\nA total transformation in our lives and a true revolution in our consciousness\noccurs when we make the giant leap from I-consciousness to One-consciousness.\nIf we were to understand One-consciousness in simple terms, it is to experience a\ndeep sense of connection with ourselves and all of life. Our sense of self includes\nus and others, us and nature, us and the earth, us and the universe itself. In One-\nconsciousness our sense of self increases and expands progressively until there is\nno circumference. We become limitless and in\u0000nite.\nOne-consciousness is not one speci\u0000c state; it is an expanding state of being.\nIn such a state of being, you tend to create an energy \u0000eld of great harmony and\npower around you, which will draw great coincidences and magic into your life.\nYou generate an intelligence that can cut through life’s challenges. You generate a\nlove that can heal any hurt. You generate wealth with which you can support\nmany more than you ever imagined possible. And a journey away from I-\nconsciousness and a movement into the various planes of One-consciousness is\nwhat we call awakening or enlightenment.\nQuestion: What is awakening?\nAnswer: The journey of evolution in consciousness has been called\nenlightenment, zazen, satori, mukti, awakening, self-realization, and many other\nnames. For simplicity’s sake, let us discuss the word “awakening.”\nBecause all our su\u0000ering states—be they of existential ennui, fear, anger, or\nsadness—are daymares: like terrible dreams you are having while you are awake.\nDo you remember waking up from a nightmare? It takes some time to realize\nthat what you were enduring was a nightmare and not reality. When you \u0000nally\nwake up, you feel great relief.\nThe ancients viewed all of those su\u0000ering states and separation-driven states\nas daymares from which we need to wake up while awake. When we do\ncompletely wake up, we smile with the joy of realization. And we wake up to\nthree progressive planes of One-consciousness: beautiful states, transcendental\nstates, and enlightened states of consciousness.\nAwakening is to wake up from I-consciousness to One-consciousness. And\nwe will dwell more on it another day.\nQuestion: In the book, we only read about beautiful states. What are the other two\nplanes you speak of? And aren’t there only two states of consciousness: a\nsuffering state and a beautiful state?\nAnswer: It is true there are only two states in which we ever live: a su\u0000ering state\nand a no-su\u0000ering state. There is no third state.\nIf we observe su\u0000ering itself, we see it can begin as unpleasant states of\nboredom, irritation, indi\u0000erence, or tension. Su\u0000ering can also intensify into\nstates of discontent, anger, fear, insecurity, sorrow, or loneliness, and snowball\ninto obsessive states of existential ennui, desperation, depression, hate, or\ndespondency.\nWhen it comes to no-su\u0000ering states, again there is a spectrum. We categorize\nthem into three major planes of experience: beautiful states, transcendental\nstates, and enlightened states. In each of these planes of consciousness, your\nexperience of life is di\u0000erent. Consciousness is an ocean with endless shores. We\nchose to speak of one shore in this book: the beautiful state. Let us talk very\nbrie\u0000y of these three planes.\nBeautiful states of consciousness are not extreme emotional states of high.\nThey are states that are characterized by an absence of con\u0000icting internal\nthought noise. In beautiful states you experience a greater sense of connection\nwith yourself, with the other, and with the world. You are present to life.\nCalmness, connection, love, compassion, joy, serenity, a\u0000ection, gratitude, and\ncourage are all beautiful states. It is possible for every one of us to live the\nmajority of our lives in a beautiful state. As our brains, bodies, and\nconsciousness go through a transformation, even if su\u0000ering does arise, we can\nquickly dissolve it and return to a beautiful state.\nRapture, bliss, universal love, peace, equanimity, fearlessness—these are\ntranscendental states that do not last for prolonged periods. When we rise to\ntranscendental states, we become witnesses to the movement of life. We are in a\n\u0000ow with life. We realize that the trees, the earth, humans, and every form of life\n\u0000ow into us and we \u0000ow into them. We are inseparable from all life. These are\nout-of-the-ordinary states that are experienced in deep meditations and\nprocesses. We awaken to the mystic in transcendental states. In these states some\npeople also have transcendent visions or extrasensory experiences. At the\nacademy, we notice that these intense states often result in life-changing shifts.\nIn the enlightened state of consciousness, you wake up from the duality of\nmatter and consciousness, sacred and unsacred, you and the other, divine and\nhuman, su\u0000ering and pleasure. You awaken as the one. Enlightened states are\nknown to leave a permanent imprint on human consciousness.\nHaving seen such states, would not our experience of everyday living itself go\nthrough a radical shift? docs/edge-cases-research.md:327:| **Exploitation Vector** | User: "Tell me about meditation. आगे हिंदी में बताओ." LLM might: (a) respond in English still, (b) switch to Hindi but lose thread, (c) hallucinate Hindi translations of spiritual terms. |
docs/edge-cases-research.md:335:| **Description** | Sarvam AI or similar transliteration engine incorrectly converts between scripts (Devanagari, Tamil, Telugu). Sacred mantras or spiritual terms are transcribed incorrectly, changing meaning. |
docs/edge-cases-research.md:338:| **Mitigation** | Maintain a curated dictionary of sacred terms with correct script renderings. Validate transliterations against known spiritual vocabulary. Offer both original and transliterated versions. User can flag/correct transliteration errors. |
docs/edge-cases-research.md:382:| **Potential Impact** | **Critical**. False negative: user in crisis receives no help. False positive: user feels surveilled/uncomfortable, privacy violated. Legal liability if a user self-harms after an inadequate response. |
docs/edge-cases-research.md:383:| **Mitigation** | Tiered escalation: spiritual distress → mental health triage → crisis resources. Use dedicated crisis detection model (not general LLM). Human-in-the-loop for high-confidence crisis flags. Clear privacy disclosures: "If we detect you're in danger, we may share this with crisis services." Test distress signals against diverse spiritual vocabularies. Provide emergency resources prominently. Never dismiss or spiritualize genuine mental health crises. |

docs/transcript_audit_research_notes.md:9:Apple describes WER as an industry-standard ASR accuracy measure but notes that it lacks nuance because it penalizes insertions, deletions, and substitutions equally. The article introduces HEWER, which emphasizes major errors affecting meaning, readability, and proper nouns while discounting minor filler-word and spelling variants. The article reports an example where average WER was 9.2% while HEWER was 1.4%, illustrating that readability/semantic risk can differ materially from aggregate WER. Implication: this corpus audit should treat deterministic structural checks as necessary but insufficient; high-risk doctrine terms, proper nouns, and meaning-changing substitutions need targeted review.

docs/mukthi_quality_research_notes.md:5:- `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qiBib0ap0KM` (Building Graph Memory for AI Agents with LangGraph & Neo4j) describes a dual-memory design: thread-scoped short-term memory plus user-scoped long-term memory in Neo4j. Memory nodes use a key, namespace derived from user/thread identity, value, and timestamp. Retrieval is user-scoped, ordered by timestamp, and injected into a dynamically rebuilt system prompt. The demonstrated privacy test changes the user ID and verifies that memories do not leak across users. The video emphasizes an explicit write/read path and UI testing across session restarts.
docs/mukthi_quality_research_notes.md:37:Opening `/second-brain` while unauthenticated correctly redirects to `/auth`. The auth surface is visually coherent and exposes sign-in, sign-up, password recovery, diagnostics, and privacy links. A full end-to-end Second Brain UI check requires an authenticated session; no credentials were requested or entered. The code path is therefore reviewed statically and the unauthenticated privacy boundary is confirmed visually, but authenticated vault provisioning, unlock, recall, forget, and export still need a dedicated logged-in E2E run before being considered fully verified.
